# cyphersolver

An informal benchmark of frontier AI models on historically "unsolved" ciphers, as much a hobby as a measurement.
Most of the work is by Claude (Opus 5 and 5.5, some Fable 5.1); a few results came from outside contributors using
other models. Each target's `profile.json` records which model did what, and what it was given. The first targets
came from three standard lists: S. Tomokiyo's
[Unsolved Historical Ciphers](https://cryptiana.web.fc2.com/code/unsolved.htm), Klaus Schmeh's
[Top 50 unsolved encrypted messages](https://scienceblogs.de/klausis-krypto-kolumne/the-top-50-unsolved-encrypted-messages/),
and Elonka Dunin's [famous unsolved codes](https://elonka.com/UnsolvedCodes.html). Since mid-September 2026 most come
from this project's own catalogue of undeciphered archival letters (DECODE records, BnF and Gallica notices,
Tomokiyo's "undeciphered" letters, printed calendars). Each one is a test of how far an AI assistant, working with a
human, can get on a problem that has resisted people for decades or centuries: how much is archival research, how much
is cryptanalysis, and where it stops. Method: archival research, historical cribs, printed editions, and small
purpose-built solvers, always run against matched controls so that a negative result says something. The results
are being written up with George Lasry as a paper on how LLMs perform against historical ciphers.

- **Website:** https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/ — hub, priority queue, and formal write-ups (source in [`docs/`](docs/)).
- **Solved, catalogued:** [SOLVED_CATALOGUE.md](SOLVED_CATALOGUE.md) — every item solved, read or partly read, the method that broke it, and where the AI did the work.
- **Solved, ranked:** [SOLVED_RANKING.md](SOLVED_RANKING.md) — the finished targets in one table, scored by difficulty, historical weight, novelty, research effort, profile and verification.
- **Catalogue of open targets:** [CATALOGUE.md](CATALOGUE.md) · [site page](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/catalogue.html) — undeciphered letters and series absent from the standard lists, scored for importance, solvability and difficulty. Generated from [`catalogue.json`](catalogue.json); a target leaves it when it is read, resolved or found in print. How it was compiled and refilled (BnF/Gallica, DECODE, the 22 Sept 2026 new-solves sweep) is in CATALOGUE.md; harvest scripts in [`research/catalogue_harvest/`](research/catalogue_harvest/).
- **Per-target record:** each folder under [`targets/`](targets/) has a `NOTES.md` (sources, dead ends, what is established and what is inferred) and a `profile.json` (the fixed-field record behind the paper; [`profile.schema.json`](profile.schema.json)).

## Results

The third column of each table is the day the finding landed in this repository: the date of the first commit
that carried it, taken from the git history, not the date of the document. The write-up pages carry the same
date, and `docs/_dates.json` holds them all. "Date" is the date of the document itself.

### Key recovered from ciphertext-only

Key rebuilt here from the ciphertext alone. Independent re-solutions of items solved elsewhere are marked.

| Target | Date | Landed | Extent | Result | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Desiderio l’Abbé → Duke of Nevers, Prague and Breslau, BnF fr. 3198 nos. 30, 37; second copy no. 38 (catalogue #284, class C) | 2 Mar and 2 May 1577 | 7 Oct 2026 | complete | **Key recovered by AngusRobinson (PR #20)** from the ciphertext with the probable word *l’empereur*: homophonic figures, vowels 1–15, consonants two-figure groups, 6 = word space. All 31 runs deciphered, 534 of 536 tokens (99.6%); checked here against the images, the 23 Sept draft (98.1% of figures agree) and 1,000 shuffled keys (z = 12). Polish court politics in March; in May, of Weber: “mais plusieurs ne pleureroient s’il n’y retournoit jamais”. Two blotted figures restored from context. | [`targets/labbe1582/`](targets/labbe1582/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/labbe1582.html) |
| **George W. Erving → James Madison**, Madrid, private No. 24 "Duplicate", "In Mr Pinkneys Cypher, the Cypher of ye Legation", NARA RG 59 Despatches from US Ministers to Spain vol. 10 = M31 reel 12 frames 0362–0367 (NAID 188605361; Founders Madison/99-01-02-1993, early access; not in the catalogue or on DECODE; sibling of [`targets/erving1807/`](targets/erving1807/)) | 10 Aug 1807 | 4 Oct 2026 | complete | **Key recovered from the Founders text and the code of the NARA duplicate; contributed by Feyseel Nur (PR #21), with Claude Opus 5.5 subagents; adversarial review by OpenAI Codex.** Founders prints the letter decoded, with ten blank gaps and without the groups; the duplicate on the film has the groups and no decode. 1,145 groups transcribed and aligned with the Founders text: Pinckney's legation code rebuilt to 414 rows, partly alphabetical (870–880 *their … thing*, 1340/1341/1343 *an/ance/and*). Checked against Jacob Wagner's 1803 decode of Pinckney's own despatch (M31 reel 7 frame 0348): 45 of 47 shared groups agree, 584 and 943 undecided. 1,125 of 1,145 groups (98.3%) give sense without the 11 conjectures (1,136 with them); Codex: confirmed but overstated, 716 groups (62.5%) have outside support (Wagner, the March letter), 908 (79.3%) recurrence or outside support, 0 digit errors in 53 sampled groups, and a conservative count without hidden-digit restorations and emendations of 1,081/1,147 (94.25%). Six of Founders' gaps filled from the code (*that government*, *and*, *hostile*, *resort*, *to Hanover and*, *these*), *expectations* plausible, *past* and *pride* guesses; three disagreements (*very [?] authority* for "best informed persons", *the vast [3 groups] Spain by the war* for "costs brought about", no *with*). Godoy paid for the Hanover bargain "out of his own funds", is "deceived and furious", and "as a dernier resort he will make an alliance of some sort with England!". Open: 633; 632, 455, 1482; 767, 679, 378; one group in the binding; 916 in the postscript; two uncertain positions on frame 0367. | [`targets/erving1807aug/`](targets/erving1807aug/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/erving1807aug.html) |
| **Archduke Leopold Wilhelm → the abbé (baron) de Mercy**, instruction in cipher from the camp at Barneton, BnF Espagnol 144 f. 22r–v (Gallica btv1b10035717h; finding-aid item 11; not in the catalogue, GitHub issue 16 by NoAutopilot) | 6 Jun 1648 | 1 Oct 2026 | partial | **Key recovered from the ciphertext alone by NoAutopilot (cipher-lab); transcription checked on the images and corrected here.** A homophonic substitution of the numbers 2–34 inside clear Spanish; 521 of 529 cipher tokens read (98.5%, measured). The four "uncertain codes" 48, 52, 65, 72 are digit pairs (4 8, 5 2, 6 5, 7 2) and two 26 are 2 6; the square-with-a-dot sign is the comte de Saint-Ibal (the clear instruction of 13 Apr on f. 21r asks that Santibal come with Mercy); "su camarero mayor" is Konrad von Burgsdorff, Brandenburg's chief chamberlain. Mercy is to go to Cleves and ask the Elector whether 3,000 infantry in two or three regiments can be raised (the substance of Lonchay et al. IV no. 183). Open: eight tokens of f. 22v line 4 (code 15) and the letters lost at the trimmed edge of f. 22v | [`targets/mercy1648/`](targets/mercy1648/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/mercy1648.html) |
| **Otto von der Malsburg, general war commissary → Landgrave Wilhelm V of Hesse-Kassel**, ten cipher letters on the war in Westphalia, Hessisches Staatsarchiv Marburg, HStAM 4 h Nr. 1411 ff. 3–33 (HCPortal 496–509, all "Not solved"; catalogue 338, class C) | 15 Jan – 28 Mar 1637 | 28 Sept 2026 | partial | **Key recovered from the ciphertext alone; the letters deciphered.** No Malsburg key among the 176 HCPortal key records of the Marburg key volumes (harvested). German homophonic table of 89 two-digit groups (2–7 homophones per letter) annealed from three letters, all restarts converging; digraph signs (N ch, Z st, W ss, L tt ...) valued from context. 9,338 of 9,736 cipher groups (95.9%) decipher to sense, 97.9% of the non-code text (shuffled-key control 50–58%); 236 doubtful groups re-checked on the scans. Garrisons without pay, Ehrenbreitstein, safe-conducts refused for the Cologne peace talks, the Dutch alliance. Also a French letter signed "232" and London news extracts in the same key. The separate ten-line alphabetic block on f. 12 now has a recovered period-five key, contributed by Larry Beck with ChatGPT: shifts 4, 5, 3, 6, 2 over 24 letters. Its selected transcription contains 510 alphabetic positions; its critical reading is partial and is not included in the numerical 95.9% measure. Open: about 40 numerical code groups, alphabetic groups 43, 45 and the angular sign labelled LL, and local manuscript readings. | [`targets/malsburg1637/`](targets/malsburg1637/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/malsburg1637.html) |
| **Sienese envoys and others → the government of Siena**, the cipher letters of the Concistoro, Archivio di Stato di Siena, Concistoro 2308 fasc. 2 "Lettere in cifra" (DECODE R4790–R4814, all dated "1500–1599", no status; keys R4746–R4789; catalogue 336, class B) | before 1429 to 1547 | 24 Sept 2026 | partial | **Keys recovered from the ciphertext alone for three letters, and from a fragment on the letter for a fourth.** None of the 43 filed keys fits any letter. No. 25 (a lord's route by Lajatico, Radicondoli and Casole into Sienese territory): 477 of 496 signs (96.2%). No. 18 (Latin treaty articles with the Emperor, subscribed Niklas Ziegler, war on the Florentines at Ponte a Valiano): 1,271 of 1,459 signs (87.1%). No. 14 (envoys writing from Rome while Carlo Malatesta, d. 1429, was alive): 58.6% in sense, 29.2% doubtful. No. 4 (Galgano Borghesi to Leonardo Benvoglienti, Naples, 12 May 1456): 114 of 144 signs, key from an alphabet fragment below the text and the recipient's glosses. Nos. 8, 10, 13/16 and 22 deciphered at the time. Open: Meister's 1421 letter (no. 1) and nos. 2, 26–28, not photographed; two long systems (nos. 6 + 24, 20 + 23); nos. 7, 9, 11, 15, 17, 19, 21 without a key | [`targets/siena1421/`](targets/siena1421/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/siena1421.html) |
| Matheo de Segura → Constable of Castile, Madrid, BnF espagnol 336 no. 99 ff. 196r–198r (catalogue 321, class B) | 8 Aug 1596 | 23 Sept 2026 | complete | Both short passages; all 125 cipher tokens proposed, 119/125 (95.2%) excluding six tentative assignments. Homophonic key rebuilt by annealing and image resegmentation. The world ending or the king dying by September, and Ponte/Aponte with Escudero. No independent key; punctuation and name segmentation editorial. Previously the last item listed undeciphered in Tomokiyo’s es. 336 list after Lasry’s 2023 solutions (ff. 69/81 printed keys, f. 101 interlinear decipherment, f. 148 near-monoalphabetic); Devos 1950 full tables not obtained. | [`targets/segura1596/`](targets/segura1596/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/segura1596.html) |
| Giovan Gioacchino da Passano (attributed), London → Francis I, BnF Clairambault 331 ff. 156–157 (catalogue 281, class C) | 15 Mar 1530 (copy heading) | 23 Sept 2026 | complete | **Surviving account deciphered.** Dotted re-transcription overturned the failed attempt: cash, credits, annual revenues, jewels, gold/silver vessels, furnishings, Winchester, St Albans and York. 395/402 signs assigned; 386/402 coherent (96.0%) after 13 explicit emendations. Three rare signs remain open; a page and three lines omitted by the historical copyist are absent and unread, outside the denominator. Partial key; no independent plaintext. | [`targets/joachim1530/`](targets/joachim1530/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/joachim1530.html) |
| **Unknown Catholic gentleman of the Agenais → the connétable de Montmorency**, "Mémoire en chiffre du XIIe decembre 1560", BnF fr. 3157 no. 67, ff. 152r–156v (catalogue gives f. 158; Gallica btv1b90598645 views 153–158; catalogue 276, class C) | 12 Dec 1560 | 22 Sept 2026 | complete | Lasry solved it independently in 2023 (improved Feb 2026), unpublished (private communication). **Solved: key broken here ciphertext-only; read (98.9% of 1,715 cipher words, measured).** Ten pages wholly in a cursive pseudo-script, no decipherment or key anywhere. Homophonic substitution with word division, two-stroke units (zo = u, pp = m, s3 = r, lz = d), polyphonic δ (u/b/g) and four word codes (Z = pour, 4 = roy, c+ = et, F = sieur); the transcription corrected against the images with the key (about 300 x→r fixes). A report on the Protestant rising in the Agenais a week after François II's death: 2,000 at Clairac, the Caumonts and their ministers, Biron turning back from Bergerac, the rebel captains at Nérac around Navarre ("le chief"), the Agenais deputies to the États of Orléans. Open: 16 scattered words; the writer's name (not in the text). | [`targets/memoire1560/`](targets/memoire1560/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/memoire1560.html) |
| Unknown author → unknown recipient, "L'Estat du Roy de Navarre et de son party en France", after 1580, BL Harley MS 1582 ff. 263–264 (DECODE R8505, "unknown sender", Non-decrypted, language "English?"; catalogue 121, class C) | after 1580 | 22 Sept 2026 | complete | **Cipher broken here from the ciphertext alone; read (95.1% measured).** The catalogue item's other record, R8501, is a Stafford 1586 despatch already read from its own f. 72r decipherment (`targets/harley1582r8500/`). R8505 is this French memoir on Navarre's party, identified here from its clear text. Homophonic alphabet of ~50 graphic signs: **the letter m is a group of two, three or four dots**, taken for punctuation by every earlier pass, which is why no key fitted. A control experiment (real French under a key of the same shape) located the fault in the solver, not the manuscript: with a wrong Sukhotin constraint and the temperature scale fixed, the control solves 100%, and the real text broke at once. Reads: the prince of Parme "pour lui rendre les places", "un party de malcontens", an "italien qui sert d'ingenieur a Lisbone", "une armee de reistres", Beaucaire sur le Rosne, Béziers, Carcassonne, Montmorency, d'Épernon. Open: ten nomenclator numbers (names; 72 is a country, almost certainly France, 81 a person, probably the King) | [`targets/harley1582r8505/`](targets/harley1582r8505/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/harley1582r8505.html) |
| Ferenc Wesselényi, Palatine of Hungary → the King (Leopold I, inferred from "Maiestatis Vestrae"), Latin draft with cipher runs, MNL OL E 199 (Wesselényi family archive), 8. pallium 1. 01 (DECODE R674, "non-decrypted", dated 1 Jan 1664 = start of a 1664–1670 span; catalogue item 45, class C) | c. 1663–64 | 22 Sept 2026 | complete | (241 of 244 cipher tokens, 98.8%, measured). Broken ciphertext-only: a Latin anneal allowing two numbers per letter, then hand correction from the clear context. The cipher is a reversed alphabet, 24 = a … 3 = x, with vowel homophones 25 a, 26 e, 28 o, 29 u; DECODE key R672 (Teleki collection keys no. 12) has the same alphabet, found afterwards, but its syllables and codes do not fit. The general [60] should join the regiments of Heister, Schneidau and Sporck, add 2,000 foot and camp at [64] so that it takes His Majesty's garrison, by force if need be: "ista nux durissima … frangi debet". Struck corrections: Wesselényi's draft. Open: codes 60 (the general, perhaps de Souches) and 64 (the place), no key | [`targets/wesselenyi1664/`](targets/wesselenyi1664/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/wesselenyi1664.html) |
| Sir Simonds D'Ewes, private cipher log of his son Clopton's convulsion fits, BL Harley MS 286 f. 61 (DECODE R7759, "unknown sender to unknown recipient, 1635"; BL catalogue: Harley MS 289 f. 61r; catalogue item 130, class B) | 3 Apr 1635 – Jan 1636 | 22 Sept 2026 | complete | (96.5% of 287 words, measured). D'Ewes's own invented alphabet, simple substitution of Latin; key rebuilt by crib (convulsio, plena luna, difficilis), weekdays and moon phases checked, Autobiography confirms 3 Apr 1635 start and 44 fits. Open: last line trimmed under the mount, one word of l. 13, a faint phrase in ll. 24–25. | [`targets/harley286/`](targets/harley286/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/harley286.html) |
| **Unknown writer → Hesse-Kassel**, Hessisches Staatsarchiv Marburg, HStAM 4 d Nr. 1218 no. 49 (DECODE R4500, "partially decrypted"; catalogue 153, class B) | undated (volume 1635–52) | 22 Sept 2026 | complete | Digit-and-sign letter substitution (12 = r, θ4 = ch, a few homophones), no sibling key in the volume's ~120 key sheets; annealed on de-1500s, confirmed by the contemporary gloss on lines 1–4. ~97 % read (report to the Emperor and the Elector of Bavaria, "damit ich aus allen fall entschuldigt sein"); nine signs at the end of line 1 open, under the faded gloss | [`targets/marburg1635/`](targets/marburg1635/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/marburg1635.html) |
| **Ferdinand of Bavaria, Elector of Cologne (Bonn) → Duke Maximilian I of Bavaria**, ten ciphered pages, BayHStA, Kurbayern Äußeres Archiv 4591 ff. 281–286 (DECODE R9426, "non-decrypted", no author, no recipient, no place, no language) (catalogue 165, class C) | 22 Dec 1619 | 22 Sept 2026 | complete | (95.8% of cipher tokens measured). Broken ciphertext-only: a homophonic substitution on the even two-digit figures 10–78 (vowels with two or three homophones, consonants one, k and w only in the upper series), two signs for word division, and a three-digit nomenclator alongside; key recovered by annealing 2,482 letters against `lang/de-1500s`, with ~60 single-digit transcription slips repaired (0/6, 3/5, 1/4, 2/7) and a sample re-checked on the images. Control: the key reads the sibling R9425 unchanged ("Albrecht von Gottes Gnaden"); on 2 Oct 2026 R9425 (ff. 278–280) was transcribed and deciphered in full with the key unchanged, 97.3% of 1,567 cipher tokens measured: Archduke Albert's written resolution of 15 December 1619 for the League's agent Licentiate Morreus (Bohemia to be recovered, his troops to join the Catholic army, no muster place or 500 horse because his levies go to Luxembourg, Breisach referred to Oñate, the 1,000 cuirassiers to Ambrogio Spinola); it shows 414, 415, 416, 417, 419 to be nulls and fixes 242 = *und*, 165 = *Mt.*, 2 = *Ambrosio*. The record's "unknown" sender is named by the clear last page — Bonn, 22 December 1619, signed Ferdinand — and by the ciphered "mein Thumbcapitel", "meine Landtstendt", Paderborn. Six weeks after Frederick V's coronation: quartering companies in the county of Horn, at Montfort and at Deutz, two Kopfstück or two Ort of a Königsthaler a day, Lintelo's and Herberstorff's levies, a year's respite for Paderborn, and the argument that unless the Catholics act they will shortly have nothing left to secure them against "Einfal, Gwalt und Betrangnus". Not in *Briefe und Akten* NF I/1 or any other edition searched. Open: 31 of the 32 nomenclator groups (only 248 = *und* established; no key for this cipher survives in the volume), three unidentified names, four words | [`targets/ferdinand1619/`](targets/ferdinand1619/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/ferdinand1619.html) |
| Charles of Egmond → grand master of France, Arnhem, BnF fr. 3015 no. 8, fol. 16 (catalogue 28, class C) | 18 July (?), year unknown | 20 Sept 2026 | complete | **Independent re-solution, not a first break** (Lasry 2023). **Read, with minor uncertainties; already solved by Lasry in 2023** (key on Tomokiyo's GL.htm §2, pointed out by Lasry 21 Sept 2026), so this is an independent re-solution. Sixteen cipher lines, two insertions and the address recovered as French with an inferred substitution key and nulls. Requests support after entering war on the king’s commands and assistance for the commander of Saint John. Date sign and a few literal anomalies retained; no independent key or clear-copy control, no personal recipient name or year established | [`targets/egmond/`](targets/egmond/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/egmond.html) |
| Swatow telegram to Sun Yat-sen (JACAR B03050738800) | 1916 | 15 Sept 2026 | complete | Systematic code condenser over the standard telegraph code recovered by brute force; 41 of ~44 characters read | [`targets/sunyatsen/`](targets/sunyatsen/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sunyatsen.html) |
| Sun Yat-sen's intercepted telegrams, JACAR 特定人ノ発受郵便物取調 (Mar 1916 – Mar 1917) | 1916–17 | 18 Sept 2026 | partial | The Japanese Ministry of Communications' copies of Sun's circle's wires: ~60 condenser telegrams read under seven keys of the Swatow family (Sun ↔ Yamada, Chen Qimei, Qingdao, Manila, San Francisco, Dai Jitao 1917), plus the Hankow +111 digit code; Chen Qimei's assassination as reported to Tokyo; Sun's six-vowel 文密 code still open | [`targets/sunyatsen/y1916/`](targets/sunyatsen/y1916/NOTES.md) · [`targets/sunyatsen/y1917/`](targets/sunyatsen/y1917/NOTES.md) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sunintercepts.html) |
| Warsaw, 24 Dec 1627, to the Bishop of Olmütz? (DECODE R1408, ÖStA HHStA) | 1627 | 16 Sept 2026 | complete | Read in full from Tomokiyo's transcription: a homophonic substitution whose alphabet turns out to be in plain order (odd figures a–m, even figures n–z), with letter pairs for consonants, a dozen two-figure syllables, the letters *a m* as nulls and thirteen word codes. A 5-gram + dictionary annealer converged from random and seeded starts, shuffled controls failing. The writer, back in Warsaw from Nikolsburg, presses for a promised canonry of Olmütz for one of the Queen of Poland's sons. 2 Oct 2026: the DECODE image checked (Tomokiyo's hapax 12 + null o is the code 120; a gap on line 10 is a 29, *stimato*; a docket at the foot names Card. Dietrichstein, the addressee), measured 316 of 332 significant tokens (95.2%); the sibling candidate R1409 (f. 178) is a different system (the R1408 key ranks 67th of 500 random keys on it, a ciphertext-only attempt did not converge), and none of the fascicle's key records R1392–R1406 is this key. Ten word codes and two small groups stay glossed from context only; no key material | [`targets/warsaw/`](targets/warsaw/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/warsaw.html) |
| Henry of Navarre → Ségur, BnF 500 de Colbert 401 ff. 233, 239, 288v (listed as Henry III) | 1585–86 | 16 Sept 2026 | complete | Lasry solved it independently in 2024, unpublished (private communication). Three figure-cipher letters to the envoy raising a German army, read from the Gallica images. The upper figures fall into blocks of five (mod 5 test), an alphabetical syllabary; a structured annealer with the letters' clear French as context recovered a key that is alphabetical on its own (a 4–6 … u 45–47, x y z, et, null; syllables ba–vu 54–123; doubles ée ll ss), 97 % on a matched control. Conclude with Duke Casimir, raise the largest levy and march it; Clervant's two thousand reiters; the Vivarais or Dauphiné road. Word-signs partly glossed; f. 143 (1583) is another key. The whole volume swept: one more cipher leaf, f. 366 (autograph, same key, mostly names), and f. 333 read with Tomokiyo's sibling key, which is this key shifted by eight | [`targets/segur/`](targets/segur/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/segur.html) |
| Cardinal Soglia → nuncio Viale Prelà at Innsbruck, ciphered dispatch printed in *L'Italia del Popolo* no. 40 (Milan, 30 June 1848; Roman broadsheet reprint; Cipherbrain 13 Dec 2014) | 1848 | 18 Sept 2026 | complete | (18 Sept 2026): 5 = word separator, a 64-cell two-digit table of letters, homophones and syllables, 8XXX = one-part code in alphabetical order; Joe's transcription with one digit restored. A counter-order: stay with the Emperor at Innsbruck and suspend the earlier order to ask for passports after Lützow's departure; to be shown to Mgr Morichini. The DBI has the nuncio leaving Innsbruck on 14 June, a day before. Ten code words read from context and checked by alphabetical rank; 11 code groups open (the date of the earlier letter among them) | [`targets/soglia1848/`](targets/soglia1848/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/soglia1848.html) |
| Gottfried van Swieten (Bonn) → Count Cobenzl (Brussels), three despatches with ciphered passages in numbers, 1757–14 Jun 1759, ARA Brussels, Secrétairerie d'État et de Guerre 1236 ff. 141, 327–328, 344–345 (DECODE R955–R957, "non-decrypted"; catalogue item 39, class C) | 1757–59 | 22 Sept 2026 | complete | (22 Sept 2026). R955's 89 groups are deciphered in clear on the same page (Hanau magistrates and neutrality) and show an alphabetical syllabary. The 1759 letters use a new table (100–442), rebuilt ciphertext-only: monotone annealing against a French LM (one seed in eight broke it), then hand correction from the clear text round each run; four DECODE runs re-read from the scans. 96% of 553 groups read: the Gueldre convention and Moers, Soubise's army living on Gueldre contributions, Broglie the only competent general, complaints against Count Pergen. | Johann Heinrich Kauderbach (Saxon-Polish resident, The Hague) → Brühl / Friedrich August II, ten intercepted despatches with passages in figures, 23 Dec 1754 – 25 May 1756, KHA The Hague, Prins Willem V inv. 201 (DECODE R1043, R1044, R1954, R1958, R1959, R2078–R2082, "never solved"; catalogue item 216, class C) | 1754–56 | 19 Sept 2026 | complete | (19 Sept 2026). Broken ciphertext-only on 18 Sept (contributed, PR #4), then re-read line by line from the page with the key in hand: the system is a **reciprocal letter-pair substitution** in ordinary cursive letterforms (a/i b/p c/f d/q e/t g/u l/y m/z n/s o/r h/x), two dots for a doubled letter, a null word-divider, a few special signs, and syllable/word figures (31 = *ma*, 139 = *vous*, 145 = *me*). The earlier 44-symbol key hid this because the transcription merged e with c and b with h. 790 of 1,105 cipher tokens read (the 18 Sept key gave the same words for 40.6%): *j'ay esté infiniment mal satisfait du peu d'execution … de tant de belles promesses … pionniers et munitions … le juste mescontentement … affin que 141 ne me delaissent tout à coup … pour y commander et conserver la plaine … de ramener mon armée ès quartiers de la Faulche … vous tiendrés les chemins … ma resolution, le peu de moien*. The pencil pigpen key on the flap (akt·blu·cmw/…) reads nothing in any orientation. The cipher is a homophonic pigpen (cell shape + 1–3 dots, inner-stroke variants; several signs for e, i, s, t, r). Hand transcription of the last page (450 signs) → quadgram solver finds French → key completed from words. Last page reads nearly throughout: *je ne pouvois recevoir d'un ennuy plus sensible, ayant appris que du mariage de ce gentilhomme … l'entiere affection que j'ay vouée a vostre service … et vous supplie tres humblement, monsieur, me continuer l'honneur de vostre bienveuillance et croire que je vous honnore*. **2 Oct 2026**: ff. 241 and 242 hand-transcribed and decoded. The letter runs **f. 243 → f. 241**; **f. 242 is a second copy of f. 243**, line for line. f. 241 closes it: an envoy to congratulate *Sa Majesté* on recovering from a long illness, *le zele que j'ay a son tres humble service*, signed "Nicle"(?), *De Nancy le [..] decembre*. Measured: **84.0% of the letter's signs read as sense** (f. 243 81.7%, f. 241 86.5%; the copy 73.5%); key extended (H3 = z, C*2 = q, C1 f/q and F2 l/h polyphones). Neighbouring records R922–R933 opened: none in this pigpen. **3 Oct 2026**: every sign of every unread word cut out at 3–4x and re-read (corner sides, dot counts, inner strokes, two writer-cancelled signs); F*1 = l, D*1 = o; a joint re-anneal over-fitted and was not adopted. f. 243 now reads *lequel salua dernierement [name] de la part de [name] mon maistre, vous aviés creu quelques choses au prejudice de l'entiere affection …* **843/882 signs read as sense (95.6%)**, f. 243 98.3%, f. 241 92.5%. ### Key recovered based on plaintext from external sources

Key rebuilt by matching the ciphertext against a plaintext found elsewhere (an edition, a calendar, a copy in another archive).

| Venetian ambassador at Rome → the Doge and Senate, ASVe IT ASVe 0045 010 Busta 27 f. 354 (DECODE R1862, "unknown sender (Roma) to unknown recipient", Non-decrypted, no transcription; catalogue 263, class C) | 29 Apr 1628 | 22 Sept 2026 | complete | **Read: the cipher is R1874's, and the key was rebuilt from that record's contemporary decipherment.** R1874's decipherment (~4,800 letters) aligned to its cipher by soft-EM gave a syllabary grid (prefix = vowel 6 a / 51 e / 62 i / 53 o / 64 u, last two digits = consonant), which reads this despatch unchanged. 1,280 groups transcribed here from the images (the record has none, and one manuscript line on page 3 had been missed entirely); 99.1% have a value and 0.95 of the text reads as sense (0.92 by a stricter window, 0.96 by hand audit). Cardinal Barberini asked twice whether Palma (Palmanova) was fortified and the Pontebba pass secured; the ambassador urged that the Pope go in person as legate to settle Mantua and Savoy. The word band proved alphabetical, which bracketed the open codes and corrected three values inherited from R1874 (628 sa, 6439 signor, 6481 i). Open: 12 word-code tokens (no other letter in this cipher survives to fix them) and one image-true line; the ambassador is not named. | [`targets/r1862/`](targets/r1862/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r1862.html) |

Key rebuilt from plaintext beside the ciphertext: a partial interlinear or marginal decipherment, clear passages, a deciphered copy in the same file.

Two runs beside clear words broke it — *seiscientos* after "los", *en tiera* after "puso" — and the alphabetical order then predicted the unseen values (f 21, x 41, y 42, z 43), each confirmed on *effetos*, *baxeles*, *yban*, *plazer*. The viceroy of Sicily, a week after the Piccolo Soccorso got into Birgu, reports a **second relief run waved off**: the galleys came within four miles of the harbour mouth, the *barqueta* sent ahead arranged the fire-signals, and La Valette signalled *clarissimas* that they must not enter, twice within the appointed hour, so they slipped back out to sea undetected — as *Mosiur de Lenni* (Andrea Provana di Leinì) and the rest report, with Çanoguera piloting. Also: La Valette embracing Cardona's 600 soldiers and weeping, unable to speak; the forbidden sortie that killed 200 Turks; Turkish lines "seven, eight and ten palms high"; 1,500 Turks at the water and sickness killing men in the Ottoman camp; the Algiers squadron off Cabo Bono; 40,000 escudos a month for the ships, half the 48,000 ducados already eaten, and *El estar sin galeras me destruye*. The companion covering letter of the same day, AGS Estado leg. 1394 no. 249 (PARES 3576586, *duplicado*), was deciphered in full on 2 Oct 2026 with the key unchanged, 150 of 150 cipher tokens (two new signs: barred θ = *ll*, an overlined M-like sign = *r*): La Valette's latest letters are *harto mas apretado* than expected, the Algiers ships off *Cabo Bono*, the galleys for *la Goleta* held for the *respuesta del Maestre* and leaving *mañana*. The records are Wotton's own drafts in clear English; persons and places are one- and two-digit code numbers. Cribs *durch* and *und* let the annealer write *nach der Oberpfaltz und gegen die [442]*; the key was extended word by word from there: *etwa auch die in Schlesien agirende [361] dazu ziehen*, *gute acht geben*, *Fürsichtigkeit nachvolgen*, *durch Espionen*, *wohin er seine Marche zu richten*. The crib was bound with it: **R9644 and R9650 each carry the clerk's own full contemporary clear version** beside the cipher, keyed by marginal letters A/B/C. **47 code values fixed** against that plaintext, 29 probable (kept apart; an audit produced 4 retractions, and the 10 substitution-alphabet signs were later downgraded to probable when an 8× zoom showed signs that must be different letters looking near-identical at the magnification they were read at — the code groups, plain latin trigrams that cross-check, are unaffected). The key reads where it was not built: 70%/62%/57% of tokens in R9648/R9650/R9645, and *el papa* unaided in R9656, which has no crib. Structure not in the published account of the siblings: homophones at code level (`ta`/`tu` both *no*), codes carrying syllables (*na·da*, *que·da·rian*, *con·ocia*) and codes running together (*porque*, *quales*). Read complete: R9644, R9648 (its duplicate), R9650, R9652 — Adrian VI temporising between Charles V and Francis I, the French into Italy with a great sum to Lyon, Adorno's intercepted letters, the Pope *tan escasso que nunca hombre lo fue mas*. **Correction to DECODE**: R9652 carries no cipher at all. **2 Oct 2026**: R9649 deciphered in full from its own clear (f. 268; the Ostia fortress, the licenciado Bernardino); the key extended on **R9634, which is from Genoa, 13 Sept 1522** (DECODE has the month only): Geronimo Adorno's plan for Charles V and Henry VIII to invade France through **Provence**, *la tierra mas flaca que el tiene*, 70,000 ducats a month, set for March — 846/1191 tokens read as sense (71%); then R9645 73% (f. 243r = the Ferrara capitulation terms), R9646 80%, R9656 61%, all measured. **3 Oct 2026**: codes pooled over all 21 Hurtado letters, spelled words matched against a Spanish lexicon, R9656 re-transcribed from the images: R9634 872/1191 (73.2%), R9645 75.3%, R9656 177/225 (78.7%), 76% over the five; still partial (DECODE has no larger image; the RAH Biblioteca Digital is behind a bot check). New values: `ɋ` = r, plural `z` after codes, `yob` ducado, `xu` gente, nulls after the opener `Ꮒ`. | Pieter Battier → Gaspar Fagel, Madrid, NA 3.01.18 inv. 401 (109 scans; named key NA 1.10.29 inv. 1209, DECODE R2794) | 1686–1688 | 4 Oct 2026 | partial | **Read with known key.** Two letters pass strict sense without a known contemporary decipherment: 24 Apr 1687 (58R–59L), conservatively 230/235 (97.9%); 8 Apr 1688, 313/323 (96.9%), conservatively 312/323 (96.6%); 322 key-attested. 19 Dec 1686 is the third validation letter: four passages, 186 tokens, reproducing the contemporary list on 32R apart from spelling and gedaan/afgedaen; this strengthens the inv. 1209 key identification. 4 Dec 1687 (94.3%) and 12 Feb 1688 (92.3%) remain below the strict bar. The first two validation letters give 1113/1132 comparable tokens (98.3%; 94.7% key-alone); review is software, not paleography. | Prince de Conti → Laigue and Noirmoutier, Paris, 5, 26 and 27 March 1649, BnF fr. 3854 nos. 41–43 (catalogue item 26) | 1649 | 18 Sept 2026 | complete | (18 Sept 2026). Nos. 41–42 were found already read (Lasry, HistoCrypt 2023, a poly-homophonic letter cipher; his paper miscites "fr. 3584"). They are re-read here from a fresh transcription, adding *ne* pouvant, *pour insister* and the B‹l›etaigne slip. No. 43, supposed to be their crib, is a different syllabic number code: four interleaved alphabets, five-syllable blocks, underline = reversed syllable, 99–250 nulls. Its clerk never glossed the first 12 lines (264 groups). The key was rebuilt from ~3,300 glossed groups; it reads the opening, *Pour Monsieur de Legue …*, and nine structure/context values were then confirmed by glosses elsewhere. The whole mémoire is edited: Conti to Laigue, 5 March 1649, the day after Rueil opened, promising the Archduke to break the conference the moment he enters France | [`targets/conti1649/`](targets/conti1649/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/conti1649.html) |

Clear Spanish with cipher passages (1,716 groups), each deciphered in the margin at the time. Aligning the margins showed the cipher to be the Balbases 1677 syllabic nomenclator; that key reads all four unchanged, verifying the margins and recovering words DECODE could not read (four values added: 167 Suecia, 161 resolución, os para, 6+ o). Content: the Swedish–Danish treaty project, Hamburg and Brandenburg, Sweden's embassy playing for time against French demands, the senate deciding on armed aid to France. Second pass on the images (22 Sept 2026): the 118 groups first left unread were DECODE misreadings (62), code groups fixed by the margin (48), nulls (7); one illegible, 99.9% of 1,716 groups valued (99.3% without 11 uncertain); 13 = e and underlined N = V.S. corrected; DECODE omits two lines of R1012 | [`targets/hernannunez1674/`](targets/hernannunez1674/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/hernannunez1674.html) |
| Galeazzo Visconti → Angelo Bolano (Alessandria) and → Francis I (pieve de Locate), BnF fr. 3045 f. 28 no. 24 and fr. 3096 f. 91r no. 47 (DECODE R4230, R4256; catalogue item 179, class C, filed as "Français 3045 (2 items)") | 15 Oct 1528; 15 June 1529 | 22 Sept 2026 | complete | (1408 of 1477 cipher signs, 95.3%, measured; to the King 667/699 = 95.4%, to Bolano 741/778 = 95.2% after a push pass; upgraded from read in part 22 Sept 2026. Thin margin: without two judgement calls, a doubled-R *loro* and a dotted p read U from sense, the Bolano letter is 94.6%). Lasry's 2023 key extended: a bare Λ doubles the next letter, far = CHE or NTO, ρ = F, θ = D and G, q = H (F to the King). Both clear closes read "ali XV", not 12 as in DECODE and Tomokiyo. The Venetians fear the peace; Visconti urged forcing Milan ("il tuto, Sire, è a uno fine non bono"); Savona relieved, "come batale perduta, ogniuno voleva andare ale stancie", infantry gone for want of pay. | **Catherine de Médicis (Chenonceau) → Villeroy (in Guyenne with Anjou)**, BnF fr. 15564 ff. 30–34, cipher block on f. 33r (DECODE R4157, catalogued as "unknown sender (Villeroy) to unknown recipient, 5 Feb 1587"; catalogue item 173, class B) | 7–8 Feb 1581 | 22 Sept 2026 | complete | (all 289 signs accounted for: 262 read as sense, 27 nulls and padding). DECODE's sender, recipient and date are wrong: the docket reads *Double de la lettre de la Royne mere du Roy à Mr de Villeroy … febvrier 1581*, and the letter is printed in *Lettres de Catherine de Médicis* t. VII pp. 349–353, the cipher silently replaced by the contemporary marginal decipherment. The eight lines read with Satoshi Tomokiyo's reconstructed key (Cryptiana, fr. 15564 f. 33), split into finer sign classes and extended by twelve values: 18 = *dict*, 4/b = *nous* (not *vous*), C̄σ = *mais*, ß̄ = *la*, ō = *Angleterre*. The prince Dauphin to take the Low Countries' oath of fealty for Anjou, Condé to lead the forces, the embassy to England. Tomokiyo's unread last line is *d'Angleterre* followed by 20 padding signs. Open (graded M): ō = Angleterre, the e of *Cond[é]*, the missing r of *pouoir* | [`targets/r4157/`](targets/r4157/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/r4157.html) |

Unseparated two-digit figures read with the key in Nevers's papers, BnF fr. 3995 f. 64 "Per Cavare 1590" (Tomokiyo's no. 35), applied unchanged; beam decoder with the it-cinquecento model for the writer's 3/9, 0/8, 7/8 look-alikes. No prior decipherment. The rumour that Sixtus V was poisoned by the Spaniards, hope for Henri IV's conversion, Mantua's devotion to the French crown against the common enemy. Read sign by sign in three passes with his key, ~12 letter-sign corrections and code values harvested from the letters deciphered at the time (fr. 3983 no. 80, fr. 3984 f. 182, fr. 3985 f. 23) and from context in BnF esp. 336 nos. 79–80: no. 11 ~714 of ~720 letter signs and all 58 code tokens (41 H, 11 C, 6 M); no. 62 recto 607/617 tokens (98%), all 62 codes (36 H, 13 C, 13 M), verso ~70/101 under bleed-through; no. 100 all 1,000 tokens (codes 65 H, 26 C, 19 M). Mayenne wants the crown or the regency; Spain pays 40,000 écus and assignations to 260,000, offers four millions; the Cardinal de Bourbon offers his daughter's marriage; Mayenne's council le [archevesque] de Lyon (Épinac), Villeroy, Jeannin; the Infanta plan judged impossible; Feria's jurist mocked; a truce proposed; money through Zamet; Feria and Tassis hint Spain may turn to Savoy. Not in the published table and proved on the leaves: a null, the crossbarred long-s = *e*, a double-circle nomenclator for *duca*, ꝓ = *d*, ⊡ = *ll*, ≠ = *cc*. No. 66 (docketed *duplicata*) enciphers different stretches from no. 65, so the pair cribs itself. Alfonso d'Este *risolutamente concluse per cosa dil mondo non voler per sé alcunamente accettare il regno et manco far l'impresa a suo nome*; he will accept only the accord the King makes for him with the Pope, pleads the want of provisions, and the agents fall back on Ercole d'Este and Trivulzio | [`targets/sormano/`](targets/sormano/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sormano.html) · [second write-up, by Arya Sanketbhai Patel](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sormano1529.html) |

Mostly clear Latin with about three pages of cipher, and the cipher is where the politics is: the French are trying in many deceitful ways to break the Habsburg–Ottoman peace, for no other reason than that, not daring to attack Charles V with their own forces, they want it done at the Sultan's charges; Rüstem Pasha is not to doubt Habsburg sincerity. The money passage (pp. 5–6) reads almost continuously: 2,800 ducats of arrears and 1,500 for the year from 1 March, carried by the secretary Justus de Argento, plus a credit of up to 3,000 repayable at Venice against Malvezzi's note. | Charles Hémard de Denonville, bishop and then cardinal of Mâcon (Rome, Orvieto) → Anne de Montmorency, eight ciphered letters, BnF Français 3053 ff. 6, 16, 21, 32, 35, 40, 77, 85 (DECODE R4233, R4234, R4235, R4238, R4239, R4240, R4247, R4248; catalogue 180, class A) | Sept 1536 – 6 Apr 1537 | 21 Sept 2026 | partial | **The “unknown sender” identified and every cipher passage of the eight records deciphered, seven in part; the eighth resolved.** The catalogue had eight letters to the grand maître from an unnamed correspondent at Rome. Three are signed in clear *Charles e. de Mascon* / *E. Card[ina]l de Mascon*: the volume is the despatches of Francis I's ambassador to Paul III. The cipher is the one Tomokiyo reconstructed from fr. 3053 itself and Lasry re-tabulated in 2023 (used in `targets/gramont1529/`), with **no plaintext published anywhere**, so the work was reading. Homophonic substitution on graphic signs, doubled-letter signs, two nulls and exactly three code numbers (20 = l'Empereur, 30 = pape, 40 = roy — no other number occurs). Read: R4240 ~85% (Vergerio's message to the Germans, the Pope's volte-face on the general council), R4247 letter 1 ~80% (Andrea Doria's galleys, a suspected secret treaty with the Emperor, Milan refused to the Farnese), R4238 ~65% (the Sienese exiles, a German offering Ferdinand's secrets), R4233 ~60% (the Farnese marriages, a conclave weighed while Paul III fails, a design on Milan), passages of R4235 (*le dict pape vouloit entretenir le roy de mariage … pour ce s'en prevaloir vers le dict Empereur*) and of R4239 (Ottavio Farnese's match with Margaret of Austria, the Florentine exile cardinals, an *honneste estat en France* offered to Pier Luigi Farnese against the Imperial offer of Novara), and 66 lines of R4234. **R4248 is not in Mâcon's cipher at all** but a second fr. 3053 key, whose full contemporary clear decipherment stands on f. 86; read from that. Six contemporary marginal or interlinear decipherments found in the volume served as controls, three agreeing word for word; one of them corrects the published table (the big curly X is Q/U, not N). A third pass the same day read every remaining page: the rest of R4234 (Pier Luigi's troops held in Parma and Piacenza to garrison Milan if the Emperor sells it; Ambroise *corruptible plus que tous les hommes*), R4235 P2-P3 and passage D (against a bound clear copy), R4247's second letter (Pitigliano: strike at Naples) and the 100 unread lines of R4239 (Novara offered at the Pope's command). About three quarters of the cipher letters were then read. **Pass 5 (22 Sept)**: the readers' one barred-cross alias proved to be four or five distinct key signs; every record re-transcribed by sign shape from the Gallica native scans and decoded with the key + a French LM, checked against every clear copy (ff. 37r-v found to be the clear copy of R4239 letter 2; Dupuy 44 companion letter to the King): **~95 % of all cipher letters read**, CER 3-8 % vs contemporary clear texts; R4247's 'faded' tail is sharp on Gallica; 'Lodes' is Lodi, not Rodez. Open: ~5 % ambiguous sign runs, some names; the f. 85 key not tabulated | [`targets/rome1536/`](targets/rome1536/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/rome1536.html) |

| Catherine de Médicis and the Court → Bernardin Bochetel, bishop of Rennes, ambassador at the imperial court: BnF français 3181 f. 55; Cinq cents Colbert 390 pp. 139, 357; Colbert 392 p. 231 (catalogue 11, class B) | Dec 1562 – 1564 | 20–21 Sept 2026 | partial | **Four letters never deciphered, read in part (79% of 2,308 signs measured as sense, 15% probable; 5 Oct 2026).** Bishop of Rennes’ cipher (Tomokiyo’s reconstruction): homophonic, ~30 word-signs, clear-looking word nulls. Each letter is in a different secretary’s hand, so each hand’s forms were fixed on letters in that hand that carry contemporary decipherments (fr. 3181 f. 57–58; Colbert 390 p. 138, pp. 189/199, 221–231), and each line decoded by a language-model lattice over the candidate values (`lattice.py`, `fr-1530-despatches`). **f. 55** (Catherine, 31 July 1563; La Ferrière II 79–81 printed the cipher as *« Partie chiffrée »*): ~91%, the Council of Trent and the Habsburg marriage — *pour le bien de la Chrestienté et avancer le concile… ce qui se promect des sessions de Decembre… vous estant dernierement à … Trante*; f. 57’s margin reads *par France*, not *par Ferrare*. **Colbert 390 pp. 357–358** (1564, after Ferdinand I’s death; p. 358 carries the end of the cipher): ~99% — drop the precedence quarrel on another pretext, *laisser là quelque secretaire… soubz couleur d’aucuns voz affaires particuliers*. **Colbert 392 p. 231** (Bourdin, Paris, Dec 1562; clear end on p. 232): ~85% — encloses Catherine’s letter of 15 Dec 1562 (La Ferrière I 448–451) on the secret marriage overture, which *sçavoit incontinant en Espaigne*; *[s]a femme … triumphe de la premiere ouverture… de ce mariage*. **Colbert 390 p. 139** (*Deschiffrez vous mesmes… et la mettez au feu*): ~65%, three code words for persons/places unread — *que je vous tienne pour trop advisé et affectionné et loyal serviteur du roy mon fils… les yeux ouverts pour observer… mon cousin*. Calibration drew on every decipherment found in Colbert 390–395 (full ones on pp. 120–121, 199, 313). | A Jacobean codebreaker's file of Italian intercepts, TNA State Papers 106 box 10, ff. 128–195 and 202–205 (DECODE R657, R660, R664, R704, R720, R721, R722, with the key R725; worksheets R658–R702; catalogue 56-adjacent, class B) | c. 1623–24 | 20 Sept 2026 | partial | **The seven records are one case, dated, and the cipher explained; the letters read in fragments.** DECODE lists seven anonymous, undated, partly decrypted ciphertexts ("1558/1625", the volume's span). They are one English codebreaker's working file: intercepts with his solutions written between the lines, his worksheets, and — catalogued three records later as a *key* rather than a ciphertext, which is why it fell outside the target list — **the fair copy of the cipher he reconstructed (R725), transcribed here in full**. System A: two-figure groups read three ways by diacritic, in the codebreaker's own words, "there are numbers of woordes expressed by this difference of — over the figures" — bare = a letter (A 10/11, B 31, C 41, D 25, E 20/90 … Z 93), barred = a word of one list (16 *lettere*, 18 *relatione*, 37 *Re d'Inghilterra*, 52 *liga*), ticked = a word of a second (22 *Il Principe*, 23 *Marques Inichiosa*, 24 *Imbassiatore di Venetia*, 32 *Roma*, 34 *Palatinato*, 90 *Papa*, 91 *Nuncio*), plus written letters standing for whole words (a = *per*, g = *il*, q = *et*, z = *che*). **Dating**: that nomenclature — the Marqués de la Inojosa in England 1623–24, Cardinal Francesco Barberini from Oct 1623, Palatinato, Il Principe — fixes the file to **1623–24**, against the catalogue's sixty-seven-year span. **Correction to DECODE**: the file holds three ciphers, not one (R657 two-figure; R660/R664/R720 three-figure; R704/R721/R722 letter-and-figure), and R720 is filed with R704 but is written in the three-figure code of R660/R664. About **45 values of the three-figure code** rebuilt by aligning the codebreaker's interlinear syllables on R664 f. 1, checked by the word *senza* running over the line break (252 se + 359 n + 560 za); *della loro sicurezza*, *et hoggi terzo giorno quelle de nuovo senza*, and in the clear *potrete continuare nel modo cominciato, osservando però il [mutamento]*. Open: neither long letter read through; systems B and C not carried to a continuous reading; R657 not worked against the R725 key; no sender, recipient or codebreaker named **Catalogue 62 (21 Sept 2026):** seven more records of the box (R658, R661, R663, R667, R684, R701, R702) are the same codebreaker's worksheets, not intercepts; R658 is his own count-and-value grid of the three-figure code, which R701 calls "the Venetian Cifer", and it rebuilds that code as a systematic syllabary (consonant × a-e-i-o-u; 117 values, 102 in his hand), checked on his glossed strings on R702 (46/46 groups: *i colonelli*, *sendosi*, *che possiamo prometter*) and correcting four earlier values (260 ta, 262 ti, 263 to, 459 r); a second three-figure code and a short letter cipher on R701 stay unread. | [`targets/sp106box10/`](targets/sp106box10/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sp106box10.html) |
| Cardinal Francesco Barberini (Rome) → Giovanni Battista Ceva, nuncio extraordinary in Paris, two letters, AAV Segretario di Stato, Francia, doss. 346 nos. 2 and 11 (DECODE R75, R84; catalogue 248, class A) | 25 Sept, 18 Dec 1632 | 20 Sept 2026 | partial | **Two undeciphered letters of the dossier deciphered.** The entry listed five ciphertexts as a transcription job; in fact George Lasry reconstructed the key in 2020 and read four of the eleven letters, covering three of the five, while no reading of R75 and R84 was published. System: two-figure homophones and syllables, every `2x` a null, `6` a word separator occurring inside no code, and three-digit nomenclator elements (a prefix digit plus an ordinary code, not the four digits the DECODE note claims). Beam search over the unseparated stream under `it-cinquecento` interpolated with a model built from the dossier's own clear text; leave-one-out against Lasry's four letters, 95.8% of tokens, nomenclator recall 0.70. Controlled against the 1632 interlinear on R75: 1,105 letters, 77.5% agreement. Content: 25 Sept, the Swedish victories make *l'accomodamento* easier and *l'unione* harder, and the reach of that power *sino al Mediterraneo* is a danger to France; 18 Dec, a month after Lützen, the fear of *conseguenze che tengano disunita per un lungo pezzo la Francia … impotente a soccorrer li suoi alleati*. Six nomenclator elements read (474 mente, 495 quanto, 498 tanto, 830 Francia, 854 guerra, 149 piazza), the first solved in this dossier. Open: 117 nomenclator elements, which hold every name; transcriptions not re-checked against the images | [`targets/ceva1632/`](targets/ceva1632/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/ceva1632.html) |
| Marquis del Vasto → Charles V and the letter to "Garbino", BnF fr. 3022 nos. 6, 10, 20 (catalogue item 7, class C) | 1527–28 | 18 Sept 2026 | partial | (18 Sept 2026; no. 6 measured 22 Sept). No. 6, catalogued as an anonymous report, is del Vasto's own letter of early December 1527. It is **read, 98.0% of 1,680 cipher tokens measured**, with Lasry's Cipher 2 key and code groups identified here from context (doz = Lautreque, deh = la Marca, dnb = dinero, bet = dello …). Lasry's syllable signs were corrected against the manuscript (boz = esto, bar = tan, bopt = ellos). It reads as his plan to secure Siena and take Perugia and Urbino. Ten code groups (11 tokens) and nine short stretches remain open. No. 10 (Ischia, 27 Sept 1527) was already read by George Lasry (2023), text edited by Tomokiyo (2026); 47 of its 1,146 words are still queried, and it was not reworked here. No. 20 (Madrid, 11 Apr 1528, to "Garbino") is in Hieronimo Ranzo's initial-letter code. The numbering was tested and is not alphabetical. All 1,315 groups were transcribed, plus ~2,600 from Ranzo's letters in fr. 2988. A word annealer validated on a held-out control recovers only function words, so no. 20 needs Ranzo's table or a clear copy. No French decipherment exists (the Clairambault copies are undeciphered). | [targets/vasto1527/](targets/vasto1527/), [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/vasto1527.html) |
| BnF **Espagnol 318** nos. 5, 92, 93, 94, 95, the Catholic Monarchs' ciphered letters 1497–1504 (catalogue item 9, class C) | 1497–1504 | 17 Sept 2026 | partial | uernar*, *por obra creo que … amigo*, *escreuir a*, *dificultad*. Two of Lasry's signs corrected. **Nos. 93 and 94** stay unread but are named: both are secretariat ciphers (homophonic sign alphabet + one-part CVC nomenclator), their code initials fall outside Escrivà's key and outside the *Cifra general* and inside the band of the *Gran cifra* of 1501–04 (`lnq` against Bergenroth's LUQ = capitán). The letter-run alternative was tested and rejected: 1,016 signs of f. 118r anneal to −3.5 nats/token against −1.8 for real Spanish, and 66 % of `p` signs sit inside `pie`. Blocked on BNE MSS 20.211/52, bot-checked at every endpoint and absent from Europeana and Hispana | [`targets/esp318/`](targets/esp318/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/esp318.html) |
| Nicolas Raince → Montmorency, Rome, 13 May and 20 Nov 1526, BnF fr. 2984 pp. 29–31 and p. 105 (catalogue item 5, class C) | 1526 | 17 Sept 2026 | partial | **86 of about 106 lines deciphered** (17 Sept 2026). Not a cryptanalytic problem: Tomokiyo published the 1526 key in 2020 with no plaintext, but reading which glyph sits under which letter in his table *image* by eye slips a column — the first pass here had l, m and n each one place wrong and H under r, which corrupted every reading built on it. Re-measured off the image (header letters located, ink blobs assigned to the nearest column, every one within 15 px of a centre), the table resolves a control line glyph for glyph with nothing left over: *s [null] qui estoit en la court de Savoye [null] est party pour venir icy*. 86 of the 106 lines then read by hand off the microfilm against numbered-token images, gaps left as gaps; 20 lines carry a machine decode only. The 13 May letter, nine days before the League of Cognac: the Castilians and the Bourguignons, *deliberé prendre le chemin de Valence*, a capitulation for which *ilz seront cause de la destruction … de la part d'avoir faict capituler*, *mondict seigneur l'Admiral luy avoit dict que seroit celuy qui y tiendroit*, *la peste qui est au logis*, *des advis qui viennent aux imperiaulx par leurs*, and a man taken *un jour, en plaine place pres du palais, un nommé Pietro Anthonio*. Its clear close, transcribed here, expects Andrea Doria at Civitavecchia within a day with six galleys. The 20 Nov letter, two months after the Colonna raid, turns on a pontificate that *seroit la cause de la totale ruine de sa maison*. One glyph, ten occurrences, always at a clause boundary, is in no published table. Gallica leaf numbers run two apart because they are page numbers on odd pages, not because half the volume is unfilmed | [`targets/raince/`](targets/raince/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/raince.html) |

Tomokiyo's Nevers cipher no. 46 (fr. 3995 f. 87, read from the full-resolution Gallica image; two-digit alphabet with homophones, barred/plain/dotted word lists, 1 as an intercalated null, checked against the office's decipherment of a Gondi letter in the same key) resolves every figure of both Pisany letters: 8 Sept reads whole ("l'on dissuadoit [le Roy] de rechercher [que le Pape] luy donnast l'absolution … qu'il n'estoyt besoin qu'il allast sans cela, parce qu'il ne feroit rien … l'on n'a volonté de contanter le Pape, que l'on n'y aille poynt"), 14 Oct in long stretches. The five letters to Revol are in the Court's symbol cipher no. 60, not no. 46: the 27 Aug copy is clear but for one line (f. 115); the cipher sits at f. 176 (2 Sept), f. 146v (7 Oct, nine lines transcribed, not read), f. 157v (9 Oct) and f. 198 (23 Oct); none read yet | [`targets/nevers1593/`](targets/nevers1593/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/nevers1593.html) |

Key extended from the letter (codes cardinal, leur, pour, faire, ie, vous, lettre, le/du) and from a full alignment of the deciphered sibling fr. 4715 f. 2 (~1,050 pairs). Reads the postulation of the grand archdeacon Brulart and the dean Frison, Mayenne's delays, 18,000 lansquenets against Parma, the Elbeuf ransom of 50,000 écus. Read: depeschera … dedans deux jours le doien; la protection; Mons. Cassin; vostre […]; Mr le [code] mon maistre a esté retiré; j'ay veu ung advis qui confirme plus particulierement que nul aultre; signature Baron. Open: three code signs and two short runs (16 signs). | [`targets/orbais/`](targets/orbais/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/orbais.html) |

Ferenc Kendi 'ter perfidus' denounced as the sole author of the 1556 revolt. | Giovanni Battista Pallotto (Vienna) → Cardinal Francesco Barberini, 28 ciphered despatches, BAV Barb.lat. 6960 (DECODE R286–R313, "Non-decrypted"; catalogue 237, class C) | 4 Aug – 29 Nov 1629 | 20–21 Sept 2026 | complete | Solved earlier by **Contents in print since 1897; key found by others.** Hans Kiewning, *Nuntiaturberichte aus Deutschland* IV/2, *Nuntiatur des Pallotto 1628–1630*, Bd. 2: *1629* (Berlin 1897), prints these despatches from the Roman decipherments (ASV Nunz. di Germania 119); confirmed here against the register (R286 = pp. 5–6 = Nr. 153; p. 13 = its *Beilage II*). **The key is the key of the sibling volume Barb.lat. 6956**, broken by Norbert Biermann and Thomas Bosbach in March 2018 and filed as documents on DECODE R215. George Lasry had found years ago that 6960 uses it (DECODE was never updated), and told us on 21 Sept 2026. The system is two-digit homophones plus syllable/word groups, three-digit nomenclator groups (200–999), and the digits 1 and 8 as nulls. Applied to DECODE's transcriptions of all 28 records, it reads running Italian: R286 opens *ho riceuto la risposta datami in scritto*, Nr. 153 verbatim. About 1,750 three-digit groups are missing from the 6956 key and stay open. This project's own attacks (crib alignment, homophonic annealing, a fixed-width corpus search over the whole edition) all failed. They did establish, correctly, that the cipher uses deterministic groups of varying length, and that mix of widths is why every fixed-width test missed. Catalogue 236 (6956) was removed as solved by others. | [`targets/pallotto1629/`](targets/pallotto1629/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/pallotto1629.html) |

Stato Spagna 364D (DECODE R154–R177, "partially decrypted"; catalogue 249, class A) | 4 Jan 1718 – 6 Jan 1720 | 20 Sept 2026 | complete | Solved earlier by **Solved and read by George Lasry, 24 Oct 2020**: his reconstructed key "ASV - S364" and his segmented decipherment of the whole Spagna 364 corpus (364C/1–32 and 364D/1–24) are filed as attachments on every one of the 24 records. DECODE's "partially decrypted" and the catalogue's "noted, a transcription job" both described the contemporary marginal gloss — a few words a page, several of them wrong — not the modern solution beside it. Measured here token by token: **33,408 of 34,243 cipher groups valued, 97.6 % read**. Added here: the gap was the nomenclator, where Lasry's key gives 268 of the 399 four-digit groups the corpus uses, leaving **189 groups in 715 tokens (11.0 % of nomenclator usage)** without a value. The key is alphabetical in runs, so each has a bracket; with the 85,000-token corpus for context **61 groups are recovered** — *promozione* (45 tokens), *ordin-*, *ciò*, *Card.le*, *port-*, *risoluzion-*, *subito*, *vascelli*, *aggiustamento*, *giurisdizion-*, *tribunal-*, *corriere*, *cifra*, *negoziat-*, *governo*, *istruzion-*, *maniera*, *bisogn-*, *truppe*, *Vicerè* — and the calendar block resolves into two parallel Gennaio–Dicembre runs (4532–4543, 4552–4563), with 4538 *Luglio* confirmed independently by "delli 12 Luglio passato, qual appunto fu il giorno della promozione" (Alberoni created cardinal 12 July 1717). Unknown nomenclator usage falls from 11.0 % to 3.4 %. Two corrections to the filed key: 4474 is *Vicerè*, not a bishop, and 4535 "Apte" is *Aprile*. Open: 135 nomenclator groups (221 tokens), and 722 two-digit tokens the 2016–17 transcribers could not read off the images. | [`targets/nunzio1718/`](targets/nunzio1718/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/nunzio1718.html) |

| Alonso Sánchez (Venice) → Charles V, and Lope Hurtado de Mendoza, 1522, RAH Salazar 9/23–9/26 (DECODE R9593–R9657, 29 records "non-decrypted"; catalogue Tomokiyo list, class B) | 1522 | 20 Sept 2026 | partial | Solved earlier by **Reconstructed and published by Satoshi Tomokiyo, [*Correspondence in Cipher of Imperial Ambassadors Alonso Sanchez and Juan Manuel (1522)*](https://cryptiana.web.fc2.com/code/AlonsoSanchez.htm), 6 Sept 2025** — alphabet, ~177 code groups and the opening lines of R9593–R9632, from the contemporary decipherment on R9605. Added here: the catalogue entry corrected (the run spans 9/23–9/26 not 9/24; every record is dated **1522**, so the July 1523 treaty is not in it; nine records are **Lope Hurtado de Mendoza**; almost all carry a contemporary decipherment, so they were unedited rather than unread). The nomenclator is alphabetically ordered (C1×V×C3, sorted by C1,C3,V), which fills the numeral run: `cab` = xv, `cef` = xxvi and `cif` = xxvii predicted and then **confirmed in text**, resolving Tomokiyo's uncertain "[xxviii?]" as `cof`. Two letters outside his coverage read in part: **R9653** (20 Nov 1522) — the siege of Rhodes in clear, the foot cipher "cartas de Francia de xxvi y xxvii del passado"; and **R9635** (16 Sept 1522) — a whole page of cipher with a patchy interlinear decipherment, "los de esta republica", "del señor infante", "ducados allende de los otros xviii mil", "mas de xv mil ducados", glossed *fuessen restituydas sus bienes*. Lope Hurtado's groups take finals (`tep`, `tap`, -z) absent from Sánchez's key — a third cipher, unreconstructed. Open: 27 records, the spelled-out letter runs on R9635 f. 20r, the head paragraph of R9653 (ink bleed), Lope Hurtado's key. | [`targets/sanchez1522/`](targets/sanchez1522/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/sanchez1522.html) |
| Alonso Sánchez (Venice) → Charles V, May–Aug 1523, RAH Salazar 9/28 = A-28 (DECODE R9670, R9671, R9672, R9674, R9677, R9689, R9690, R9767, R9769, R9770; catalogue 147, Tomokiyo list, class B) | 1523 | 21 Sept 2026 | complete | Solved earlier by **Already calendared: Bergenroth, *CSP Spain* vol. 2 (1866), nos. 549, 552, 554, 558**, from the contemporary decipherings filed elsewhere in the volume. Seven records are those despatches or their duplicates, matched by folio. The recipient is Charles V, not unknown. DECODE's "Inline Cleartext" flag marks clear passages, not a decipherment. The **1522 key is unchanged**: the code groups on five records read with `targets/sanchez1522/key_codes.tsv`. The three letters of **16 Aug 1523** (R9767 Sánchez alone; R9769/R9770 joint with Caracciolo) are not in the Calendar and are mostly clear. **R9767 read in part**: clear text in full, and every code group in the cipher resolved except `pem` and `yol`. The cipher concerns a French-leaning party in Venice and the league's money clause. ### Read from existing decipherment

A complete decipherment already existed; the work was transcription and checking.

Not a Szembek letter: it follows the death of Prince Şerban Cantacuzino (29 Oct 1688) and calls the imperial envoy "Burgundus Antidius Dunod" the impostor who misled him; the living prince is probably Brâncoveanu. He marked the four undeciphered (no contemporary decipherment, not unsolved); no reading of them was published. Cipher 1 is a simple substitution on 22 letters (a=7, e=11, f=1 … z=22; ff=66, mm=67, nn=68, ss=72); applied to f. 370 it decodes the opening **letter-for-letter, 81 of 81 characters**, against a contemporary clear copy found *independently* of the key — through the old Harlay pièce numbers (224, 226, 247, 251) that Berger de Xivrey cites — in Brienne 13 = BnF NAF 6984 fol. 102v: "le cardinal de Gondy aura passé pres de vous, il y peult ja avoir quelque jours, lequel vous aura fait…". All four letters read (Brienne foll. 102v, 104v, 171r, 193v); **f. 406 is the duplicate of f. 404**, so the four leaves are three despatches; class A, not B. **Open:** only 81 characters of f. 370 transcribed group by group, f. 373 none, and Cipher 2 keyed but not applied | [`targets/maisse1592/`](targets/maisse1592/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/maisse1592.html) |

Added: the 14 cipher stretches (88 words; 84 on surviving cipher, 4 Büchi's conjectures in the burnt margin) located on the page images and translated. L&P shown to print the words of 6 of the 14; the 1516 London abstract on the next leaf (L&P 2045, Büchi 542) confirms the ciphered content; foliation reconciled. No. 26 is not a second letter but the same letter of 13 February, carrying the same cipher with the plaintext written between the lines by a contemporary decipherer; the clear text of the two copies runs word for word, the cipher stands at the same points, and no. 25's first run opens `to 92 … 17 … 43` and its third ends `… 68 93 …` exactly as no. 26's does. The letter reads in full: the Legate's departure for Spain and "le Pape pour l'acheminement de ces troupes in la Valteline", the fear "sy le Pape entroit dans la partialité"; the peace with the Huguenots, needed because "le faix d'une double guerre ne se pouvoit plus supporter"; the Dutch ships withdrawn and the Huguenot admiral following "avec le reste de sa flotte" without the King's permission; and the prince of Piedmont's propositions, which "tendent à engager le Roy à la guerre avec l'Espagne, (et que) la Seigneurie de Venize contribue comme faict l'Angleterre et Holande" — the Savoyard push Monzón defeated six weeks later. | [`targets/herbault1626/`](targets/herbault1626/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/herbault1626.html) |

Both pairs aligned group by group: a partial table of Philip II's general cipher Cg.13 (some 70 syllables in rows of five, three ending marks, c. 50 code groups), consistent with Tomokiyo's published fragment at every overlap. Cross-reading the letters reads groups the decipherers left blank (pur respeto, mes vuestro, san negocio; bur persona, dom dicho, dur porque, pen mucho from Phelippes's interlinear f. 5), shows tur to be a miscopy of tum, and corrects the copyist's "Francia" for dun (England: the fleet's return, "entre ellos y el de Bearne", "sus armaçones") and "si se hiziesse" for the King's "si yo hiziesse". Charles orders du Croc to guide the peace, government and council around James VI, prevent the young king's marriage before France is consulted, retain Scottish affection for France, and ensure “que les Anglois n'empiètent aucune chose en Escosse.” The overlay leaves several short groups open. The Hague copy (Dutch interception) matched here: same clear opening, 550 groups for 438 words, repeated groups in the print's order (1064 Vienne ×4, 4310 Sardaigne ×3, 3042 dessein, 545 avec, 87 encore). The signed original is clear English with 61 code runs (414 groups, 242 distinct, heavy with nulls); every run aligned to the print, recurring groups constant (534 = to ×8, 678 the, 839 you, 894 him, 609 his, 1373 religion, 1219.563 Protestant, 2101 Turin). Content: not the Turin embassy but a secret mission to keep the Electoral Prince of Saxony from abjuring in Rome, and to rescue him to a Protestant country if he wished. Four corrections to the print: *valent* (not "valoris (így)"), *gentibus* (not "servis"), the closing *Speramus cito nos res nostras ita disposituros, ut meliori postea modo vos iuvare possemus* (printed as nonsense), and Fraknói's "contemporary regest" is the letter's own half-ciphered last sentence. The key re-derived here reads the 17 Oct letter (four pages of cipher, no interlinear) straight from the leaf, *j'ay esté veoir le Cardinal en ses ennuis, où que l'y ay trouvé le plus grant exemple*, as Le Grand prints it. By-product: fr. 3078 no. 3, **16 June 1529** (p. 21), is in no edition and DECODE calls it non-decrypted; its key was re-derived on the leaf from the decipherer's interlinear reading of the 22 June letter (B = o and the word signs *bien*, *fault*, *paix* added to Tomokiyo's table) and the block reads in stretches — *semblant [que] madame ne fust … pour conclure … la [name] luy ayt par[lé] … [il] fault envoyé querir pouvoir, il ne fauldroyt oublyer a le faire* — about 60 % of the signs. Scheurer 1969 unchecked. | [`targets/dubellay/`](targets/dubellay/) |

The system is characterised as a two-part homophonic nomenclator: the writer struck a 9 and wrote 7 in a recurring phrase (7, 90, 924 are homophones); no alphabetical, column or multiplicative numbering fits; a synthetic R4680-style nomenclator reproduces the profile. On that control the true key scores far below gibberish keys, so ciphertext-only was closed. No key in Arcinsys, HCPortal or DECODE (112 Marburg keys of 1710–99 listed). Second session: the two letters share one key (10 of the 20 commonest symbols in common, 2.9 expected), so they pool to 1151 tokens; third session: a numba pipeline now reads clean 507- and 1151-token controls (and a 507-token control with word-signs), yet no. 78 shows no language basin in English, French, Latin, Italian or Spanish, no. 79 sits at random level, and pooling scores worse than either letter, so the same-key claim is withdrawn; a nulls-plus-nomenclator control is not read at this length, so that design stays open. f. 52 (84 tokens) is below unicity. Design: base letters a c d f g h with figures 1-99, letters, syllables and words mixed, the 1577-78 *Zifra Prima* type (not Franceschi's N.11, whose alphabet is confined to 1-20). Letter-only and letter-plus-wildcard annealers fail matched synthetic controls (0-9 % recovery even without transcription noise), so nothing is read. The discriminators show that the seven parts of 27 February (970 letters) are one daily key. Single encipherment is excluded; for the double pass, annealing, tempering, staged scoring, Sinkhorn relaxation and EM all fail on a synthetic 970-letter control with a known key (true-key basin 8-10 swaps; fails even with the cell permutation given). A known-plaintext solver recovers the boxes from ~80 pairs, but Bletchley's decrypt of the 16 June message (Borki) does not align with its ciphertext under any spelling, layout or length tried. Needs the February decrypts (TNA HW 16/17) or the exact enciphered wording of the June message | [`targets/orpo1942/`](targets/orpo1942/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/orpo1942.html) |

| Queen María of Castile → Alfonso V, Valencia, 5 Aug 1435 (day of Ponza), ACA Cancillería reg. 3225 f. 59r (DECODE R10171, public, "non-decrypted"; oldest-cipher candidate A1) | 1435 | 18 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | 42 signs in three runs inside clear Catalan; the clear context points to the truce of Majano ("es continuada daci a tots sants"). The 1437 Montcada–Amat key (DECODE R10183) is a different alphabet; the 1429 Castile-war key (Cortès & Pons, *Saitabi* 36, 1986) does not fit as an alphabet, but its nomenclator seems to match the dotted word signs (regne d'Aragó, Catalunya, rei de Castella, Navarra): a hypothesis, not a reading. Regs 3226, 3224, 3113 and the king's 2693 swept in full on PARES (1,414 images): no cipher anywhere; the king's register shows his letter to the queen of 27 July 1435 sent "in ciffra". Crib-anchored annealing with a Catalan model gives nothing. Needs reg. 3225's other 1435 folios (ACA, not digitised), the second key of B.A.H.M. ms. 677 ff. 160–161, or a queen-side key at the ARV | [`targets/maria1435/`](targets/maria1435/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/maria1435.html) |

| Intercepted League and Spanish letters summarised for the duc de Nevers, Sept–Oct 1589, BnF fr. 3977 no. 96 (catalogue item 31; *"whether the intercepts survive in the volume is unknown"*) | 1589 | 20 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | **They do not survive, and the summary is not in cipher.** No. 96 = f. 318 = canvas 520 is a digest **in clear French**, fifteen numbered points: Mercœur asking Spain for help, powder and money carried to Nantes by Diego Maldonado *"avec le chiffre général d'Espagne"*, Mauléon and *"la lettre escripte du chiffre qu'ilz ont ensemble"*, Mendoza at Bayonne on Arques and Dieppe, Spain pressing the Pope to renew the excommunication. **Every** piece in the volume dated September or October 1589 (nos. 81, 84, 85, 89–97) is in clear, so there is no ciphertext to attack under no. 96. **What the sweep found instead: eleven *other* intercepted ciphered letters in the same volume, Feb–Aug 1589, each with its contemporary decipherment written word by word above the cipher** — nos. 29, 31, 38, 39, **43–45** (three from Mendoza to the duke of Parma, filed in a multi-folio entry a naive regex drops), 47, 48, 49, 71. Eleven complete cipher-and-plaintext pairs for Spanish ciphers of 1589; none transcribed, no key rebuilt | [`targets/nevers1589/`](targets/nevers1589/) · [write-up](https://dbourdeau.github.io/cyphersolver/nevers1589.html) |
| Zhongshan telegrams | c.1938 | 15 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | No corpus exists online; the premise of the list entry was wrong | [`targets/zhongshan/`](targets/zhongshan/) |
| Blancmesnil → Nevers (fr. 3633 f. 24) | 1589? | 16 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | No ciphertext online: fr. 3633 is not digitised (the source page's catalogue link goes to fr. 4736); the Potier-Nevers key of 1589 is the candidate once a copy exists; sibling letters with decipherments sit in fr. 3616/3621 (DECODE R9434-9448, images restricted) | [`targets/blancmesnil/`](targets/blancmesnil/) |
| 1520s superscript-digit ciphers | 1526–29 | 15 Sept 2026 | &mdash; | Blocked: DECODE and BL images need login | [`targets/superscript/`](targets/superscript/) |
| Charles II → Duke of Hamilton | 1650 |  | &mdash; | Copy order for NRS GD406/1/2197 (open) | [`targets/hamilton/`](targets/hamilton/) |
| Maurice → Rupert; royalist intercepts, BL Add MS 72438 | 1645–46 |  | &mdash; | BL volumes digitised but offline since the 2023 cyber-attack | [`targets/rupert/`](targets/rupert/) |
| Torcy → Geertruidenberg plenipotentiaries; Villars → Polignac (BL Add MS 61575) | 1710 |  | &mdash; | The ciphertext itself: Tomokiyo's transcription files are dead links, DECODE R8755/R8756 need a login, the BL volume is not digitised | [`targets/geertruidenberg/`](targets/geertruidenberg/) |

<!-- Beatrice source identification: catalogue 160. -->
Six further list entries turned out to be solved by others with nothing to add here: Perwich 1670 (Brown; Lasry, Biermann and Tomokiyo, 2025), Ferdinand III and the Cardinal-Infante (Ernst, 2017), the Milroy telegrams (Bean, 2026), the Feynman ciphers 2 and 3 (2023), the Confederate Navy dictionary code (2026) and the Mazarin–Bordeaux letter of 1654 (Lasry, 2025). They are not counted in the results above; the directories `targets/perwich/`, `targets/ferdinand3/`, `targets/milroy/`, `targets/feynman/` and `targets/barney/` hold only the pointer to the published solution.
